## 1. How every session uses this plan

1. **At start.** Read this plan after `docs/CURRENT-STATE.md` and `docs/README.md`. Find your lane in section 3 and keep to its touch boundaries.
2. **While working.** Work only on your lane's items, in the order given. If something belongs to another lane, record it in section 5 for that lane; do not change it yourself.
3. **Before parking.** Update your lane's status table (section 4) and add one line to the status log (section 6). Do it in the same admin pull request that records the release, then refresh the Drive `M3` copy.

The standing rule "Continuous Delivery Master Plan" in `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` (both repositories) makes this mandatory.

## 2. Mandates that apply to every lane

These repeat the rules in `CLAUDE.md`, which win if the two ever differ.

| Mandate | What it means |
|---|---|
| No `.zip` hand-offs | Everything lands through pull requests merged by the `automerge` label. |
| Immediate live activation | Fixes take effect on deploy: no unrequested shadow mode, off-by-default flag or delayed cutover. |
| Repo first, admin first | Rebase onto `origin/main` in both repositories. Claim spec and migration numbers above every existing and open one. Re-check the live migration head right before merge. Merge `coursefinder-admin` first, then Pilot, on green CI and a passing rolled-back `live-dry-run`. Delete throwaway branches. |
| Migration collisions | If another lane has a `live-dry-run` running in GitHub Actions, wait for it to finish (up to 15 to 20 minutes) before claiming the next migration number. |
| Guardrails | G3 consumer API frozen at 75 functions / 48 tables with no drift; 7,585 manual locks untouched; active cron jobs ≤ 140, none unregistered; $0 unapproved AI and $0 Firecrawl. |
| Live lookups and keys | No hard-coded provider lists, country arrays, intake years or API version cards. One key per (consumer, API version). |
| Closed-loop records | Before parking: request registers, `ROADMAP-ENGAGEMENTS.csv`, `TEST-REGISTER.md`, `CURRENT-STATE.md`, this plan, and the Drive `Monitoring` sync (engagements, hours summary, latest-only backup pair). |

### AU AER staged ownership — Platform Admin direction, 24 November 2026 (supersedes combined AU discovery/reader assignment)

- S1-RM-Design exclusively owns AU AER Steps 1–2: official-source identification, registration, acquisition and durable stored evidence, using the existing governed pipeline. Start with 1,401 active-course providers without a registered matrix source or active matrix and alternate official sources for the 74 previously unsuitable pages. These are discovery/assessment cohorts, not guaranteed data yields. Prioritise high-course providers, including Newcastle, Bond, UNE, Deakin, Torrens, ECU, CQU, UniSQ, Melbourne Polytechnic, SAE, JMC, Sydney Metro College and ICMS. Use up to 15 per technical batch; standing 450-provider coverage target, then entire eligible territory. No new engine, cron, spending authority or speculative source association.
- S1-AER-Claude exclusively owns AU Steps 3–5 (stored evidence extraction, provider-specific normalisation, validation and submission through existing admission gates). Start with the 98 sourced-no-active providers at the 19:55 baseline and continuously consume newly ready evidence. No broad discovery, no identical paid retries. A source defect is returned to S1-RM-Design with precise evidence. Do not alter existing valid rows. S2-AER remains the shared mapper/admission owner (Steps 4–7); S1-Build remains shared infrastructure, model routing, release coordination and register writer, not the AU discovery execution owner. NZ/CA and Scholarship assignments remain unchanged, including S1-RM-Design's already recorded Scholarship publication follow-ups.
- Handoff via existing source/run IDs and existing provider queue, not a new queue: source-ready means provider ID, official URL, source ID, stored evidence ID/hash, acquisition date, relevant qualification/level/edition and confirmed usable content; link-only, wrong-page, inaccessible or unverified source is held with exact reason. S1-RM-Design records source outcome and releases claim; S1-AER claims only for extraction, never simultaneous writes to the same provider queue row. Integration Controller authorises all production operations and shared changes.
- Baseline at 19:55: 1,543 active-course AU providers, 44 active matrix providers / 6,703 rows (including Griffith 1), 98 sourced-no-active and 1,401 no-source/no-active. Latest S1-AER pass reported no additional gain: Curtin/CDU three Mistral timeouts each; ten other batch-1 runs marked failed by lane (verify live), no unchanged retry; S2-AER owns D-R3 completeness, UWA 194 level-unmapped rows and RMIT UP shared-host hold. Record and defer individual exceptions, including ACU/UOW/RMIT follow-ups; resume after broad first pass. Preserve true approval/evidence gates. S1-Build investigates model route qualification/timeouts and records missing token/USD figures without unapproved changes.
- Measure Steps 1–2 sources identified, stored evidence-ready providers, actual coverage and gaps; Steps 3–5 attempted/completed/deferred, proposed/validated rows; Steps 6–9 actual active rows/providers/course coverage, consumer read-back and two unattended cycles at grouped checkpoints. Do not treat source, queued, proposed or held as admission. Maintain F-AER, Execution Register, existing blocker queue and M3 mirror. No new task register, no overlap, no repeat prompts per batch. This later direction supersedes conflicting older AU assignment and 45-provider wave text, without changing governance or historical records.

## 3a. Data-admission lane ownership: single writer per domain (Platform Admin, 23 Nov 2026 13:00 AEDT)

Registered by `S2-Build` as release and integration coordinator. Checked against the live system at 13:05 AEDT (rolled-back read-back: every `cron.job` row; open pull requests: Pilot #1491 only, S2-RM-Design's `s1000`) and against sections 3, 4a and 4b of this plan. This is an ownership record, not a design change. Each domain has one writer. A lane changes only what it owns; anything in another lane's column goes to that lane's owner, or to the integration owner when it is shared.

### One integration owner: `S1-Build` (was `S2-Build`)

`S1-Build` is the single writer for what every lane shares. A lane that needs a change here asks the integration owner, who makes it or approves it in one place.

| Shared item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Dispatchers and schedules | `rollup-scheduler`, `admission-rule-runner` (-b, -c), `coverage-lane-dispatch` (-b, -c), `pipeline.automation_catalogue`, `pipeline.component_register`, `pipeline.rollup_targets`, admission-rule rows, job minutes (the s978 load windows) and the 140-job cap. No lane adds, removes or moves a job itself |
| Shared builds and checks | `security.course_coverage_build_v1` (every field's coverage), `security.platform_health_check_v1`, `security.consumer_api_check_v1`, the Layer 4 item tables and `security.layer4_rule_groups_v1`. A lane proposes its field's change; the integration owner merges it |
| Consumer API | Frozen v2 and earlier (G3) and the developer communication hold for Wix and Zoho; Website and Zoho v3 serving functions (`public.website_v3_*`, `public.zoho_v3_*`). A lane's v3 field change goes through the integration owner and the developer pack |
| Migrations | Numbers are taken at merge time, strictly above the live head and every open pull request (s980 pre-flight and rehearsal). No lane holds a migration timestamp in advance |
| Versions and releases | `package.json`, `src/release-manifest.js`, release notes and the UI map are bumped only by the integration owner, at release |
| Specs | Claimed in the table below before a Pilot pull request opens; never assigned in advance |
| Shared Admin registers | `docs/CURRENT-STATE.md`, this plan, the runsheets, `TEST-REGISTER.md`, the request and engagement CSVs and the Drive mirrors. Lanes write their own entries, and the integration owner reconciles them at each release |

S2-CF and S2-GEO hold no spec numbers yet; each claims its first number here when it opens a Pilot pull request. S2-INT holds `s986`, `s987`, `s988`, `s994` and `s995`.

### File-driven delivery (design linked 23 Nov 2026)

Lanes pull batches from the shared queue by the six-step loop (`PULL -> CLAIM -> EXECUTE -> SHIP -> ADVANCE -> LOOP`). Batch sizes, `HELD` reason codes, the 24-hour stale-claim release and artifact reuse are in [`2026-11-23-file-driven-continuous-delivery-design.md`](2026-11-23-file-driven-continuous-delivery-design.md). The Platform Admin reads one daily report, `docs/monitoring/DAILY-EXECUTIVE-STATUS.md` (08:00 AEDT). Ownership in this section is unchanged.

### What every lane reports for each release

1. Canonical admission before and after (rows and distinct courses or providers).
2. Distinct-course coverage before and after, in percentage points of active courses.
3. Evidence-backed publication: every admitted value has its quote, evidence and source; nothing unverified is served.
4. API verification: read-back through Website v3 and Zoho v3 (keyed where the deployed UAT can, otherwise the same SQL path in a rolled-back read).
5. Guardrails: G3 75 functions and 48 relations with no drift, manual locks 7,585, active jobs at or under 140, $0 Firecrawl and no unapproved AI, and the developer communication hold where a consumer field changes.

### Order from here

`S2-CF` and `S2-INT` may implement their independent production slices now. `S2-GEO` owns `destination.*` (confirmed 13:13) and does read-only discovery until the Platform Admin unlocks writes after its findings. `S2-Build` proceeds with scholarship coverage (`s1001`: Charles Darwin, Lincoln, Torrens; the scholarship extraction cut-off guardrail). No lane starts a duplicate reader or changes another lane's functions. The AER resolver work S2-Build had measured on 23 Nov 12:47 to 12:58 was stopped before any file was written, because `s1000` covers it.

## 6. Global Autonomous PIM: approved design and development entry point (Platform Admin, 25 Nov 2026 18:42 AEDT)

Status: **design approved and closed** (decisions D1 to D7 and nine final corrections, 25 Nov 18:42). The architecture consultation is closed; this section is the final record. Acceptance: F-OPS.13 to F-OPS.20 in `STUDYSEARCH-FEATURE-ACCEPTANCE.md`. Mockup v3: `docs/platform/data-ingestion/mockups/pim-control-plane-mockup.html`. Earlier material (`docs/platform/data-ingestion/2026-11-25-*.md`, the M3 umbrella design) is background; where it differs, this section wins.

**Development entry point.** `S1-RM-Design` starts at step 1 of 6.3 (interfaces 1 and 2 in 6.2): read `docs/CURRENT-STATE.md`, this section and F-OPS.13 to .20; confirm the live definitions of `pipeline.field_register`, `pipeline.course_field_absence`, `security.field_gap_scan_v1` and `security.enrich_plan_start_step_v1`; claim a spec number with `S1-Build`; open the first Pilot pull request under the migration pre-flight. `S1-Build` is not interrupted: it continues AER model qualification and stays the Integration Controller.

### 6.1 Retain, extend, retire

| Decision | Component | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Retain | Enrichment planner (`security.enrich_plan_auto_v1`, `enrich_plan_tick_v1`, `enrich_plan_priority_v1`, `enrich_plan_start_step_v1`, `pipeline.enrich_plan_runs`) | The one planner |
| Retain | Standing field-gap queue (`security.field_gap_scan_v1`, `field_gap_queue_tick_v1`, `field_gap_queue_run_v1`, `pipeline.field_gap_queue_*`) | The one gap mechanism |
| Retain | Dispatcher lanes (`pipeline.field_execution_registry`), `pipeline.queue_watch`, `pipeline.on_demand_requests`, `pipeline.admin_control_events`; s768 controls (F-OPS.10 to .12) | No second worker or audit log |
| Retain | On-demand authority (Agent Operating Model v1.24): a genuine scoped request is held by account limits, reserves, its own scope and protected policies, not by scheduled shares | No blanket daily on-demand cap (D3) |
| Retain | Layer 4 Inbox, its choices and its exact backend permission checks; AER admission and rejection stay with the Platform Admin (decision A3) | No generic grouped approval (D7) |
| Retain | AI controls and their exact backend checks: qualification, route settings, activation and roll-back are separate governed actions | No rank is inferred from a screen; no permission changes (D7) |
| Retain | Meaningful-content preprocessing (lean selector and input function) | Screen shows the stored payload actually sent |
| Retain | Request Register Rules v1.3, including its status definitions; the gap register stays unshared; G1 to G4, 7,585 manual locks, cron ceiling 140 | Unchanged |
| Retain | Firecrawl scheduled ceiling of 1,500 credits a day; capacity inside it follows eligible demand, priority, account reserve and measured yield | No fixed sub-allocations (D2) |
| Extend | `pipeline.field_register` and `pipeline.course_field_absence` | Interfaces 1 and 2 |
| Extend | `enrich_plan_start_step_v1` and `enrich_plan_priority_v1` | Academic entry step; scholarship step widened; request weight |
| Extend | Data Delivery (`src/DataDelivery.jsx`, read `admin_layer23`), drawn in Layer 2/3 Build & enrich › Operations | Matrix, requests and refresh views |
| Extend | `pipeline.ai_invocations` | Minimum telemetry and outcome attribution (D6) |
| Extend | Supersession writers | Guard before replacement |
| Retire (from the design; never built) | Intake Engine v0.1 `intake-planner` lane, ledger table and "Operations › Intake" screen; the Coverage & completeness "Data delivery" tab; "Approve group"; cost per admission from estimated cost ÷ recall; the 400/50 Firecrawl share and the 500 a day on-demand limit proposed on 25 Nov 19:10 | Superseded |

### 6.2 Shared component interfaces

Names marked *new* are proposals; `S1-Build` assigns final names, spec numbers and migrations at merge time.

| # | Interface | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Applicability | `pipeline.field_register` holds, per field, the entity grain (course, course × student country, course × test, award × course, provider × edition, country × edition) and the rule for whether the field applies. One read, *new* `security.field_applicable_v1(field, entity)`, used by the gap scan, the planner and the delivery read |
| 2 | Field state: five separate facts, never one status | **Applicability** (applies or not). **Canonical completeness** (verified, partial, none): a valid manually locked value is verified and counts towards canonical coverage. **Freshness** (inside or past the refresh window; only for a held value). **Source finding** where there is no value (evidenced no-source, or unknown). **Protected hold** (manual lock, G3, licence, Layer 4): a flag that stops automatic change and never removes a value from coverage. **Processing status** (queued, running, failed, held) is a job fact shown on Scheduled jobs. Evidence stays with `pipeline.course_field_absence` |
| 3 | Planner step | `enrich_plan_start_step_v1` accepts an academic entry step that hands to the existing AER lanes; a step ends by writing interface 2 facts, never "done" alone |
| 4 | Replacement guard | *New* `security.supersede_guard_v1`: a reading is superseded only in the transaction that admits a verified successor covering the same value kinds; otherwise the writer stops with `HELD:NO_SUCCESSOR`. A check two cycles later is a second safeguard that flags for review. Nothing restores an invalid, withdrawn or protected record automatically |
| 5 | Delivery read | `admin_layer23` returns, by country and domain: applicable, canonical completeness, freshness, source finding, held count and consumer read-back count, each as its own figure |
| 6 | Requests | Request scope (country, domain, field) and weight feed `enrich_plan_priority_v1`. Status keeps the Rules v1.3 meanings (Delivered: the API the developer uses today serves the whole request; Live in v3; Partly delivered). Coverage and freshness of the request's scope are shown beside the status and never change it |
| 7 | AI telemetry (minimum) | Each `pipeline.ai_invocations` attempt keeps billed input and billed output as the only totals. Static prompt and selected content are recorded as a breakdown **of** billed input, and reasoning tokens as a breakdown of what the provider billed them under; breakdowns are never added to the totals. Each read links to the canonical row it produced. Cost per retained outcome = actual billed spend for a service and model (every attempt, retry and failure) ÷ canonical rows from those reads still held and verified |
| 8 | Control and audit | Every control writes `pipeline.admin_control_events` (actor, reason, previous state) and passes its existing backend permission check. A qualification pass activates nothing |

### 6.3 Sequence, ownership and dependencies

`S1-RM-Design` leads the foundation. `S1-Build` allocates each component to its existing owner, integrates every shared function, migration, version and register, and continues AER model qualification without interruption. No chat changes another chat's row.

| Step | Work | Owner (existing chat) | Depends on | Active hours |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Interfaces 1 and 2 (applicability and field state) for every registered field | `S1-RM-Design` | Entry point above | 2–3 |
| 2 | Interface 4 (replacement guard) as a shared function | `S1-RM-Design`; integrated by `S1-Build` | 1 | 2–3 |
| 3 | Interface 3 (planner steps) and automatic enrolment: a new country, provider or course gets its plan and gap rows with no prompt | `S1-RM-Design`; integrated by `S1-Build` | 1 | 3–4 |
| 4a | Academic entry lifecycle on interfaces 1 to 4: gap → plan → read → validate → admit or Layer 4 → publish → consumer read-back → field state → refresh date | `S2-AER` (shared admission); `S1-AER-Claude` (AU readers); `S3-AER-ChatGPT` (NZ, CA) | 1 to 3 | 3–5 |
| 4b | Scholarship lifecycle on the same interfaces, **in parallel with 4a** | `S1-Scholarship-Claude` (AU); `S3-Scholarship-ChatGPT` (NZ); shared scholarship functions through `S1-Build` | 1 to 3 | 3–5 |
| 5 | Recurring refresh (per-field refresh window; $0 change check first) and source-change recovery (a moved, changed or failed source re-enters the gap queue; the old value stays until interface 4 allows replacement) | `S1-RM-Design` | 1, 2 | 3–4 |
| 6 | Two unattended result-bearing cycles per domain, read back live and through the consumer API | `S1-Build` | 4a, 4b, 5 | 1–2 |
| 7 | Interface 5 and the Data Delivery views | `S1-RM-Design` (read); `S1-API-Frontend-Delivery` (screen) | 1 | 4–6 |
| 8 | Interfaces 6 and 7; Models & services and Layer 4 regrouping | `S1-RM-Design` (reads); `S1-API-Frontend-Delivery` (screens); `S1-Build` (AI ledger, with its qualification work); StudySearch requirements register (request scope and rules only) | 1 | 4–6 |

**Critical path:** 1 → 2 and 3 → 4a and 4b in parallel → 6, about 11 to 17 active hours. Steps 5, 7 and 8 run beside it once step 1 is merged. The s871 cohort (1,563 courses left without a reading) is a regression and recovery case inside step 4a, not the acceptance scope: acceptance is every applicable active course and award. `S1-RM-Design` keeps its AU source acquisition lane.

### 6.5 Platform Admin decisions (25 Nov 2026 18:42 AEDT)

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| D1 | Architecture and implementation direction approved |
| D2 | Scheduled Firecrawl ceiling stays 1,500 credits a day; capacity follows eligible demand, priority, account reserve and measured yield; no arbitrary fixed allocations |

| D5 | Infrastructure and toolset capacity is verified independently; development is not delayed and no capacity is bought without approval |
| D6 | Minimum AI telemetry and canonical-outcome attribution approved (interface 7) |
| D7 | Existing permissions and protected activation controls stay |

**Still protected (each needs its own Platform Admin decision):** buying compute, disk, Firecrawl or OpenRouter capacity; any change to a permission or backend check; activating a model or route in production (qualification never activates); raising the AI daily ceiling, the US$5.00 paid cap or the Firecrawl ceiling; anything touching G3, the manual locks, the cron ceiling of 140, or bulk withdrawal of admitted records; sharing the gap register.
